  "short_description": {
    "id": "Anu atau An adalah dewa langit Mesopotamia awal.",
    "en": "Anu, also called An, is an early Mesopotamian sky deity.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Anu atau An adalah dewa langit Mesopotamia awal. Anshar dan Kishar disebut sebagai orang tua Anu.",
      "en": "Anu, also called An, is an early Mesopotamian sky deity. Anshar and Kishar are named as Anu’s parents.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "anu-c01",
        "anu-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Antu disebut sebagai pasangan Anu. Versi lain menjadikan Ki pasangan Anu dan ibu Enlil. Anu sering dilambangkan mahkota atau mahkota di atas takhta. Anu sering disebut sebagai tokoh latar dalam mitos. Anu, Enlil, dan Enki membentuk kelompok tiga dewa. Nama Sumeria An dijelaskan berarti langit. Prasasti Sargon menyebut Anu dan Inanna sebagai pemberi legitimasi pemerintahannya. Dalam kisah penciptaan, Anu menawarkan upaya diplomasi kepada Tiamat. Dalam mitos Adapa, Anu memanggil Adapa untuk memberikan penjelasan. Anu digambarkan memegang Lauh Takdir sebelum menyerahkannya kepada Enlil. Saat badai, Anu dibayangkan sebagai banteng besar yang mengaum di atas awan. Penceritaan Mackenzie menggambarkan Anu mematuhi perintah Anshar. Penceritaan itu menyebut Anu takut mendekati Tiamat dan kembali.",
      "en": "Antu is identified as Anu’s consort. Another account makes Ki Anu’s wife and the mother of Enlil. A crown, sometimes placed on a throne, often represents Anu. Anu often appears as a background figure in myths. Anu forms a divine triad with Enlil and Enki. The Sumerian name An is explained as meaning sky. Sargon’s inscriptions invoke Anu and Inanna as legitimizing his rule. In the creation story, Anu offers to negotiate with Tiamat. In the Adapa myth, Anu summons Adapa for an explanation. Anu is described as holding the Tablets of Destiny before passing them to Enlil. During storms Anu is imagined as a great bull roaring above the clouds. Mackenzie’s retelling describes Anu obeying Anshar’s commands. That retelling says Anu fears approaching Tiamat and turns back.",

      "source_id": "anu-s1",
      "quote": "He is the son of the couple Anshar and Kishar (heaven and earth, respectively)",

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

    {
      "id": "anu-c03",
      "source_id": "anu-s1",
      "quote": "His consort is Antu (also known as Uras, goddess of the earth)",

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Antu disebut sebagai pasangan Anu.",
        "en": "Antu is identified as Anu’s consort."
      }

    {
      "id": "anu-c04",
      "source_id": "anu-s1",
      "quote": "He is also given as the husband of his sister Ki (earth), by whom his son Enlil is born.",

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi lain menjadikan Ki pasangan Anu dan ibu Enlil.",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anu-c05",
      "source_id": "anu-s1",
      "quote": "Anu is most often represented in iconography simply by a crown or crown on a throne",

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anu sering dilambangkan mahkota atau mahkota di atas takhta.",
        "en": "A crown, sometimes placed on a throne, often represents Anu."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anu-c06",
      "source_id": "anu-s1",
      "quote": "he is often mentioned as a background figure",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anu sering disebut sebagai tokoh latar dalam mitos.",
        "en": "Anu often appears as a background figure in myths."

    },
    {
      "id": "anu-c07",
      "source_id": "anu-s1",
      "quote": "Along with Enlil and Enki, Anu formed a triad",

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anu, Enlil, dan Enki membentuk kelompok tiga dewa.",

    },
    {
      "id": "anu-c08",
      "source_id": "anu-s1",
      "quote": "He was originally a Sumerian sky deity known as An (meaning \"sky\")",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

    {

    },
    {
      "id": "anu-c10",
      "source_id": "anu-s1",
      "quote": "At this point, Anu volunteers to go speak with Tiamat and try to resolve the problem diplomatically.",

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

    },
    {
      "id": "anu-c11",
      "source_id": "anu-s1",
      "quote": "News of this soon reaches Anu, who summons Adapa to him to explain.",

      "statement": {

    {

      "source_id": "anu-s2",

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

    "id": "Enlil, juga disebut Ellil dan Nunamnir, adalah dewa udara Sumeria.",
    "en": "Enlil, also called Ellil and Nunamnir, is the Sumerian god of air.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "enlil-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Enlil, juga disebut Ellil dan Nunamnir, adalah dewa udara Sumeria. Enlil disebut sebagai putra Anu.",
      "en": "Enlil, also called Ellil and Nunamnir, is the Sumerian god of air. Enlil is identified as Anu’s son.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "enlil-c01",
        "enlil-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Versi lain menyebut orang tuanya Enki dan Ninki, pasangan Bumi yang berbeda dari Enki dewa kebijaksanaan. Nama Enlil diterjemahkan sebagai Penguasa Udara. Nippur menjadi pusat pemujaan Enlil di kuil yang disebut Rumah Gunung. Nuska, putra Enlil, dijelaskan sebagai pembantunya. Ninlil, yang juga disebut Sud, adalah pasangan Enlil. Mitos yang dibahas menyebut Ninurta sebagai putra Ninhursag dan Enlil. Pemujaan Enlil ditelusuri hingga Periode Dinasti Awal I. Enlil menjaga Lauh Takdir yang memuat nasib dewa dan manusia. Dalam kisah Zu, burung itu mencuri Lauh dari Enlil dan terbang ke pegunungan. Dalam Enlil dan Ninlil, Enlil menyamar sebagai tokoh-tokoh yang ditemuinya. Enlil dimohonkan cuaca baik demi hasil panen. Enlil membentuk triad bersama Anu dan Enki. Dalam teks Turunnya Inanna, Ninshubur diperintahkan meratap kepada Enlil di Ekur.",
      "en": "Another version names Enki and Ninki, Lord and Lady Earth, distinct from the wisdom god, as his parents. Enlil’s name is translated as Lord of Air. Nippur houses Enlil’s central sanctuary, called the Mountain House. Nuska, Enlil’s son, is described as his assistant. Ninlil, also called Sud, is Enlil’s wife. The myths discussed identify Ninurta as the son of Ninhursag and Enlil. Enlil’s worship is traced to Early Dynastic Period I. Enlil guards the Tablets of Destiny containing the fates of gods and humanity. In the Zu story, the bird steals Enlil’s Tablet and flies into the mountains. In Enlil and Ninlil, Enlil disguises himself as the figures he encounters. Worshippers ask Enlil for favorable weather and harvests. Enlil belongs to a triad with Anu and Enki. The Descent of Inanna instructs Ninshubur to lament before Enlil at Ekur.",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "enlil-c07",
      "source_id": "enlil-s1",
      "quote": "Enlil/Ellil was a family man, married to Ninlil (also called Sud)",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ninlil, yang juga disebut Sud, adalah pasangan Enlil.",
        "en": "Ninlil, also called Sud, is Enlil’s wife."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "enlil-c08",
      "source_id": "enlil-s1",
      "quote": "he is the son of Ninhursag and Enlil.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 15",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mitos yang dibahas menyebut Ninurta sebagai putra Ninhursag dan Enlil.",
        "en": "The myths discussed identify Ninurta as the son of Ninhursag and Enlil."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "enlil-c09",
      "source_id": "enlil-s1",
      "quote": "Worship of Enlil dates from the Early Dynastic Period I",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 8",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pemujaan Enlil ditelusuri hingga Periode Dinasti Awal I.",
        "en": "Enlil’s worship is traced to Early Dynastic Period I."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "enlil-c10",
      "source_id": "enlil-s1",
      "quote": "keeper of the Tablets of Destiny, which contained the fates of gods and humanity",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Enlil menjaga Lauh Takdir yang memuat nasib dewa dan manusia.",
        "en": "Enlil guards the Tablets of Destiny containing the fates of gods and humanity."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "enlil-c11",
      "source_id": "enlil-s1",
      "quote": "Zu snatched the Tablet from him and flew away with it into the mountains.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 19",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kisah Zu, burung itu mencuri Lauh dari Enlil dan terbang ke pegunungan.",
        "en": "In the Zu story, the bird steals Enlil’s Tablet and flies into the mountains."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "enlil-c12",
      "source_id": "enlil-s1",
      "quote": "He then disguises himself as each one",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 14",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Enlil dan Ninlil, Enlil menyamar sebagai tokoh-tokoh yang ditemuinya.",
        "en": "In Enlil and Ninlil, Enlil disguises himself as the figures he encounters."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "enlil-c13",
      "source_id": "enlil-s2",
      "quote": "Enlil was an important weather god often prayed to and worshipped in hopes of fair weather for a good harvest.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 44",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Enlil dimohonkan cuaca baik demi hasil panen.",
        "en": "Worshippers ask Enlil for favorable weather and harvests."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "enlil-c14",
      "source_id": "enlil-s2",
      "quote": "Enlil, Anu, and Enki made up a triad",

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

    },
    {
      "id": "enlil-c15",
      "source_id": "enlil-s3",
      "quote": "When you have entered the E-kur, the house of Enlil, lament before Enlil",

      "id": "Sin atau Nanna adalah dewa bulan dan kebijaksanaan Mesopotamia. Nanna merupakan nama Sumerianya, sedangkan Sin dan Nanna-Suen adalah nama lainnya.",
      "en": "Sin, also known as Nanna, is the Mesopotamian deity of the moon and wisdom. Nanna is his Sumerian name; Sin and Nanna-Suen are alternative names.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sin-c01",
        "sin-c02"
      ]

    {
      "id": "Kuil dan ziggurat di Ur menjadi pusat pemujaannya. Sin disebut sebagai putra Enlil dan Ninlil. Ningal atau Nikkal, dewi kesuburan, disebut sebagai istrinya. Utu-Shamash disebut sebagai putra pasangan itu. Sin juga memiliki kuil penting di Harran. Salah satu penggambaran menunjukkan pria duduk berjanggut lapis lazuli dengan bulan sabit di atasnya. Angka 30 melambangkannya dengan merujuk jumlah hari bulan lunar. Pada bulan baru, Sin dikatakan berada di dunia bawah untuk mengadili orang mati. Dalam Perjalanan Nanna ke Nippur, ia membawa muatan persembahan dari Ur kepada Enlil. Enheduanna disebut sebagai pendeta tingginya yang paling terkenal. Sin dihubungkan dengan kesuburan perempuan dan ternak. Sumber pendamping menggambarkannya sebagai pria berdiri dengan bulan sabit. Dalam teks Turunnya Inanna, Nanna menjadi tujuan permohonan Ninshubur di Urim.",
      "en": "His major cult center is the temple and ziggurat at Ur. Sin is identified as the son of Enlil and Ninlil. Ningal, also called Nikkal, is his wife and a fertility goddess. Utu-Shamash is named as the couple’s son. Sin also has an important temple at Harran. One depiction shows a seated man with a lapis-lazuli beard and a crescent above him. The number 30 represents him through the days of a lunar month. At the new moon, Sin is said to judge the dead in the netherworld. In The Journey of Nanna to Nippur, he loads offerings at Ur to bring to Enlil. Enheduanna is identified as his best-known high priestess. Sin is associated with the fertility of women and cattle. The accompanying source depicts him as a standing man with a crescent moon. In the Descent of Inanna, Nanna is a recipient of Ninshubur’s appeal at Urim.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Inanna adalah dewi Sumeria yang dihubungkan dengan cinta, kesuburan, kelahiran, dan perang.",
    "en": "Inanna is a Sumerian goddess associated with love, fertility, procreation, and war.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "inanna-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Inanna adalah dewi Sumeria yang dihubungkan dengan cinta, kesuburan, kelahiran, dan perang. Bangsa Akkadia dan Asyur kemudian mengidentifikasinya dengan Ishtar.",
      "en": "Inanna is a Sumerian goddess associated with love, fertility, procreation, and war. Akkadian and Assyrian traditions later identify her with Ishtar.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "inanna-c01",
        "inanna-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Inanna dikaitkan dengan Venus sebagai bintang pagi dan petang. Inanna menjadi dewi pelindung Uruk. Eanna di Uruk menjadi kuil utamanya. Sebagian mitos menyebut Inanna sebagai putri Enki. Mitos lain menyebut Inanna sebagai putri Nanna. Karya penyair dan pendeta Enheduanna berperan dalam berkembangnya kedudukan Inanna. Tulisan namanya memakai lambang batang buluh berlingkar pada ujungnya. Ia sering ditampilkan bersama singa, yang dijelaskan sebagai lambang keberanian. Penggambaran Inanna sebagai dewi perang memakai baju tempur. Uraian Jeremy Black membedakan Inanna dari dewi perkawinan dan dewi ibu. Teks Turunnya Inanna mengisahkan ia meninggalkan langit dan bumi menuju dunia bawah. Ninshubur mengikuti Inanna dalam perjalanan itu. Dalam teks itu, Inanna menyebut Enki mengetahui tumbuhan dan air pemberi kehidupan. Dumuzid disebut sebagai suami Inanna.",
      "en": "Inanna is associated with Venus as the morning and evening star. Inanna serves as the patron goddess of Uruk. Eanna at Uruk is her principal sanctuary. Some myths identify Inanna as Enki’s daughter. Other myths identify Inanna as Nanna’s daughter. The poet and priestess Enheduanna contributes to Inanna’s growing prominence. Her written name uses a sign depicting a reed stalk looped at the top. She is often shown with a lion, described as signifying courage. As a war goddess, Inanna is depicted in armor and battle dress. Jeremy Black’s account distinguishes Inanna from marriage goddesses and mother goddesses. The Descent of Inanna describes her abandoning heaven and earth for the underworld. Her minister Ninshubur follows Inanna on that journey. In that text, Inanna says Enki knows the life-giving plant and water. Dumuzid is identified as Inanna’s husband.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "inanna-c03",
        "inanna-c04",
        "inanna-c05",
        "inanna-c06",
        "inanna-c07",
        "inanna-c08",
        "inanna-c09",
        "inanna-c10",
        "inanna-c11",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "inanna-c01",
      "source_id": "inanna-s1",
      "quote": "Inanna is the ancient Sumerian goddess of love, sensuality, fertility, procreation, and also of war.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Inanna adalah dewi Sumeria yang dihubungkan dengan cinta, kesuburan, kelahiran, dan perang.",
        "en": "Inanna is a Sumerian goddess associated with love, fertility, procreation, and war."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "inanna-c02",
      "source_id": "inanna-s1",
      "quote": "She later became identified by the Akkadians and Assyrians as the goddess Ishtar",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bangsa Akkadia dan Asyur kemudian mengidentifikasinya dengan Ishtar.",
        "en": "Akkadian and Assyrian traditions later identify her with Ishtar."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "inanna-c03",
      "source_id": "inanna-s1",
      "quote": "She was also seen as the bright star of the morning and evening, Venus",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Inanna dikaitkan dengan Venus sebagai bintang pagi dan petang.",
        "en": "Inanna is associated with Venus as the morning and evening star."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "inanna-c04",
      "source_id": "inanna-s1",
      "quote": "Inanna was the foremost Sumerian goddess, patron deity of Uruk.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Inanna menjadi dewi pelindung Uruk.",
        "en": "Inanna serves as the patron goddess of Uruk."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "inanna-c05",
      "source_id": "inanna-s1",
      "quote": "Inanna's main sanctuary was the Eanna ('House of Heaven') at Uruk",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Eanna di Uruk menjadi kuil utamanya.",
        "en": "Eanna at Uruk is her principal sanctuary."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "inanna-c06",
      "source_id": "inanna-s1",
      "quote": "she is the daughter of Enki, the god of wisdom",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian mitos menyebut Inanna sebagai putri Enki.",
        "en": "Some myths identify Inanna as Enki’s daughter."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "inanna-c07",
      "source_id": "inanna-s1",
      "quote": "she appears as the daughter of Nanna, god of the moon and wisdom",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mitos lain menyebut Inanna sebagai putri Nanna.",
        "en": "Other myths identify Inanna as Nanna’s daughter."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "inanna-c08",
      "source_id": "inanna-s1",
      "quote": "Through the work of the Akkadian poet and high priestess Enheduanna",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Karya penyair dan pendeta Enheduanna berperan dalam berkembangnya kedudukan Inanna.",
        "en": "The poet and priestess Enheduanna contributes to Inanna’s growing prominence."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "inanna-c09",
      "source_id": "inanna-s1",
      "quote": "Her name was written with a sign that represents a reed stalk tied into a loop at the top.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tulisan namanya memakai lambang batang buluh berlingkar pada ujungnya.",
        "en": "Her written name uses a sign depicting a reed stalk looped at the top."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "inanna-c10",
      "source_id": "inanna-s1",
      "quote": "She is often shown in the company of a lion, denoting courage",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 12",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia sering ditampilkan bersama singa, yang dijelaskan sebagai lambang keberanian.",
        "en": "She is often shown with a lion, described as signifying courage."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "inanna-c11",
      "source_id": "inanna-s1",
      "quote": "In her aspect as goddess of war, Inanna is depicted in the armor of a male, in battle dress",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 12",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penggambaran Inanna sebagai dewi perang memakai baju tempur.",
        "en": "As a war goddess, Inanna is depicted in armor and battle dress."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "inanna-c12",
      "source_id": "inanna-s1",
      "quote": "Inanna is not a goddess of marriage, nor is she a mother goddess.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 14",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uraian Jeremy Black membedakan Inanna dari dewi perkawinan dan dewi ibu.",
        "en": "Jeremy Black’s account distinguishes Inanna from marriage goddesses and mother goddesses."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "inanna-c13",
      "source_id": "inanna-s2",
      "quote": "Inana abandoned heaven, abandoned earth, and descended to the underworld.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Teks Turunnya Inanna mengisahkan ia meninggalkan langit dan bumi menuju dunia bawah.",
        "en": "The Descent of Inanna describes her abandoning heaven and earth for the underworld."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "inanna-c14",
      "source_id": "inanna-s2",
      "quote": "Her minister Nincubura travelled behind her.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ninshubur mengikuti Inanna dalam perjalanan itu.",
        "en": "Her minister Ninshubur follows Inanna on that journey."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "inanna-c15",
      "source_id": "inanna-s2",
      "quote": "Father Enki, the lord of great wisdom, knows about the life-giving plant and the life-giving water.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 13",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam teks itu, Inanna menyebut Enki mengetahui tumbuhan dan air pemberi kehidupan.",
        "en": "In that text, Inanna says Enki knows the life-giving plant and water."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "inanna-c16",
      "source_id": "inanna-s3",
      "quote": "Her husband is the god Dumuzid",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dumuzid disebut sebagai suami Inanna.",
        "en": "Dumuzid is identified as Inanna’s husband."
      }
    }
  ],

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ishtar adalah dewi utama Mesopotamia yang dikaitkan dengan cinta dan perang; sumber Sumeria menyebutnya Inanna. Kultusnya tercatat di Uruk sejak akhir milenium keempat SM.",
      "en": "Ishtar is a major Mesopotamian goddess of love and war, called Inanna in Sumerian sources. Her cult is attested at Uruk from the late fourth millennium BCE.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ishtar-c01",
        "ishtar-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Puisi cinta Sumeria menggambarkannya tinggal bersama ibu Ningal dan ayah Nanna. Utu atau Shamash disebut sebagai saudara kembarnya. Venus, bintang pagi dan petang, menjadi benda langit yang dihubungkan dengan Ishtar. Dumuzi atau Tammuz menjadi pasangan Ishtar dan tampil sebagai raja gembala. Sumber menilai Ishtar memengaruhi gambaran dan kultus sejumlah dewi berikutnya. Dalam versi Babilonia standar Epos Gilgamesh, Ishtar terutama tampil pada Lauh VI. Dalam kisah itu, Ishtar melamar Gilgamesh dengan menawarkan imbalan. Gilgamesh menolak sambil membandingkannya dengan pintu berangin dan alat pendobrak yang rusak. Setelah penolakan itu, Ishtar mengunjungi dewa langit Anu. Ishtar membujuk Anu agar meminjamkan Banteng Langit. Gilgamesh dan Enkidu membunuh banteng; Ishtar meratapi tubuhnya bersama perempuan kota. Teks Sumeria tentang Inanna, padanan Ishtar, mengisahkan perjalanan dewi ke dunia bawah. Kuil Eanna di Uruk merupakan pusat utama pemujaan dewi ini.",
      "en": "Sumerian love poetry depicts her living with her mother Ningal and father Nanna. Utu, known as Shamash in Semitic sources, is her twin brother. Venus, the morning and evening star, is associated with Ishtar. Dumuzi, or Tammuz, is Ishtar’s partner and appears as a shepherd king. The source assesses Ishtar as influencing the images and cults of later goddesses. In the Standard Babylonian Epic of Gilgamesh, Ishtar appears most prominently in Tablet VI. In that story, Ishtar proposes marriage to Gilgamesh and offers inducements. Gilgamesh refuses, comparing her to a drafty door and a faulty battering ram. After the rejection, Ishtar visits the sky god Anu. Ishtar persuades Anu to lend her the Bull of Heaven. Gilgamesh and Enkidu kill the bull, and Ishtar mourns its body with the city’s women. The Sumerian text about Inanna, Ishtar’s counterpart, recounts the goddess descending to the underworld. The Eanna temple at Uruk is this goddess’s principal cult center.",

    {
      "id": "Enki atau Ea adalah dewa kebijaksanaan, air tawar, dan kecerdasan Sumeria. Penggambaran Enki menunjukkan pria berjanggut dengan tutup kepala bertanduk dan jubah panjang.",
      "en": "Enki, also known as Ea, is the Sumerian god of wisdom, freshwater, and intelligence. Enki is depicted as a bearded man wearing a horned cap and long robes.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "enki-c01",
        "enki-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Aliran air digambarkan keluar dari bahu Enki. Nama Enki diartikan sebagai Penguasa Bumi. Ikan dan kambing menjadi simbol Enki yang dijelaskan sebagai lambang kesuburan. Enki menjadi pelindung Eridu, kota yang dianggap pertama didirikan. Catatan tertulisnya ditelusuri ke Periode Dinasti Awal III. Dalam mitologi Sumeria dan Akkadia, Anu disebut sebagai ayah Enki. Teks Babilonia menyebut Enki sebagai putra Apsu. Versi lain menyebut Nammu sebagai ibunya. Enki membentuk triad Mesopotamia bersama Anu dan Enlil. Dalam Enuma Elish, Enki menidurkan ayahnya sebelum membunuhnya. Dalam Turunnya Inanna, dewi itu mempercayai Enki dapat menghidupkannya kembali. Damgalnuna disebut sebagai istri Enki. Kambing-ikan, tongkat berkepala domba, dan kura-kura adalah simbol Enki.",
      "en": "Streams of water are depicted flowing from Enki’s shoulders. Enki’s name is interpreted as Lord of the Earth. Fish and goats are Enki’s symbols, explained as representing fertility. Enki protects Eridu, regarded as the first established city. Written references to Enki are traced to Early Dynastic Period III. Sumerian and Akkadian mythology identifies Anu as Enki’s father. Babylonian texts identify Enki as Apsu’s son. Another account identifies Nammu as Enki’s mother. Enki forms an early Mesopotamian triad with Anu and Enlil. In the Enuma Elish, Enki puts his father into a deep sleep before killing him. In the Descent of Inanna, the goddess trusts Enki to restore her to life. Damgalnuna is identified as Enki’s wife. Enki’s primary symbols include the goat-fish, a ram-headed staff, and the turtle.",

    {
      "id": "Cybele atau Kybele adalah Ibu Para Dewa Frigia dan dewi alam purba. Cybele juga disebut Ibu Agung atau Magna Mater.",
      "en": "Cybele, or Kybele, is the Phrygian Mother of the Gods and a primal nature goddess. Cybele is also known as the Great Mother or Magna Mater.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "cybele-c01",
        "cybele-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Pessinus menjadi lokasi kuil utama Cybele. Sumber menelusuri kemunculannya di Yunani pada abad kelima SM dengan kuil di Athena. Batu meteor hitam yang mewakili Cybele dibawa ke Roma pada 204 SM. Kuil Cybele dibangun di Bukit Palatine pada 191 SM. Singa pendamping Cybele melambangkan kedudukannya sebagai penguasa alam liar. Cybele dipercaya dapat menyembuhkan sekaligus menyebabkan penyakit. Patungnya memperlihatkan kereta bersinga atau takhta, mangkuk, genderang, dan mahkota berbentuk tembok. Attis disebut sebagai kekasih Cybele, sementara sumber lain menjadikannya putra Cybele. Dalam kisah yang diringkas, Attis jatuh cinta kepada seorang manusia dan memilih menikah. Cybele yang marah menimbulkan kepanikan pada tamu pernikahan Attis. Perayaan kultusnya mencakup permainan umum dan pertunjukan teater di Circus Maximus. Kultus Cybele bertahan hingga abad keempat Masehi. Tradisi Yunani memuja Kibele sebagai Ibu Para Dewa.",
      "en": "Pessinus is the location of Cybele’s chief sanctuary. The source traces her appearance in Greece to the fifth century BCE, with a temple at Athens. A black meteorite representing Cybele is brought to Rome in 204 BCE. A temple honoring Cybele is built on the Palatine Hill in 191 BCE. Cybele’s lion companion symbolizes her role as mistress of wild nature. Cybele is believed both to cure and to cause disease. Statues show Cybele in a lion-drawn chariot or enthroned with a bowl, drum, and mural crown. Attis is described as Cybele’s lover, while other sources make him her son. In the summarized story, Attis falls in love with a mortal and chooses to marry. An angry Cybele causes panic among the guests at Attis’s wedding. Her cult festival includes public games and a theatrical performance at Circus Maximus. Cybele’s cult continues until the fourth century CE. Greek tradition worships Cybele as Mother of the Gods.",

      "statement": {
        "id": "Sumber menelusuri kemunculannya di Yunani pada abad kelima SM dengan kuil di Athena.",
        "en": "The source traces her appearance in Greece to the fifth century BCE, with a temple at Athens."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cybele-c05",
      "source_id": "cybele-s2",
      "quote": "A black meteorite, representing the goddess, was brought to Rome from Asia Minor in 204 BCE.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Batu meteor hitam yang mewakili Cybele dibawa ke Roma pada 204 SM.",
        "en": "A black meteorite representing Cybele is brought to Rome in 204 BCE."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cybele-c06",
      "source_id": "cybele-s2",
      "quote": "a temple honoring Cybele would be built on Palatine Hill in 191 BCE.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kuil Cybele dibangun di Bukit Palatine pada 191 SM.",
        "en": "A temple honoring Cybele is built on the Palatine Hill in 191 BCE."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cybele-c07",
      "source_id": "cybele-s2",
      "quote": "She was the mistress of wild nature, symbolized by her constant companion, the lion.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Singa pendamping Cybele melambangkan kedudukannya sebagai penguasa alam liar.",
        "en": "Cybele’s lion companion symbolizes her role as mistress of wild nature."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cybele-c08",
      "source_id": "cybele-s2",
      "quote": "she both cured and caused disease",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cybele dipercaya dapat menyembuhkan sekaligus menyebabkan penyakit.",
        "en": "Cybele is believed both to cure and to cause disease."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cybele-c09",
      "source_id": "cybele-s2",
      "quote": "either on a chariot pulled by lions or enthroned carrying a bowl and drum, wearing a mural crown, flanked by lions",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Patungnya memperlihatkan kereta bersinga atau takhta, mangkuk, genderang, dan mahkota berbentuk tembok.",
        "en": "Statues show Cybele in a lion-drawn chariot or enthroned with a bowl, drum, and mural crown."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cybele-c10",
      "source_id": "cybele-s2",
      "quote": "Supposedly, Attis was Cybele's lover, although some sources claim him to be her son.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Attis disebut sebagai kekasih Cybele, sementara sumber lain menjadikannya putra Cybele.",
        "en": "Attis is described as Cybele’s lover, while other sources make him her son."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cybele-c11",
      "source_id": "cybele-s2",
      "quote": "he fell in love with a mortal and chose to marry.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kisah yang diringkas, Attis jatuh cinta kepada seorang manusia dan memilih menikah.",
        "en": "In the summarized story, Attis falls in love with a mortal and chooses to marry."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cybele-c12",
      "source_id": "cybele-s2",

      "statement": {

    },
    {
      "id": "cybele-c13",
      "source_id": "cybele-s2",

      "statement": {

        "en": "Greek tradition worships Cybele as Mother of the Gods."

    {
      "id": "Mitra adalah nama ilahi Indo-Iran yang terkait dengan Mitra Rigveda dan Mithra Avesta. Bahasa Avesta mempertahankan makna perjanjian untuk miθra.",
      "en": "Mitra is an Indo-Iranian divine name connected with Rigvedic Mitra and Avestan Mithra. Avestan miθra preserves the meaning covenant.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "mitra-c01",
        "mitra-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam bahasa Sanskerta dan Indo-Arya modern, mitra berarti sahabat. Dalam tradisi Persia, Mithra dihubungkan dengan matahari terbit, perjanjian, dan persahabatan. Sumber membedakan Mithra Persia dari Mithras kultus misteri Romawi. Mitra Weda dihubungkan dengan perjanjian, fajar, kesuburan panen, dan kebenaran. Pasangan Mitra-Varuna menghubungkan Mitra dengan dewa langit Varuna. Mithra Persia digambarkan menaiki kereta bercahaya yang ditarik kuda putih. Persenjatannya mencakup tombak perak, busur dan anak panah emas, pisau, kapak, serta gada. Mithra memberikan farr atau karunia ilahi kepada raja yang layak. Nama Mithra dipanggil dalam prasasti Kekaisaran Akhemeniyah. Mihr Yasht menyebut Mithra membela pihak seiman maupun yang berbeda iman dalam perjanjian. Mihr Yasht menyebut Mithra memberikan kecepatan kepada kuda. Himne menggambarkan Mithra memiliki seribu telinga dan sepuluh ribu mata. Mithra dipuji sebagai kuat, tidak tidur, dan selalu terjaga.",
      "en": "In Sanskrit and modern Indo-Aryan languages, mitra means friend. Persian tradition associates Mithra with the rising sun, contracts, covenants, and friendship. The source distinguishes Persian Mithra from the Mithras of the Roman mystery cult. Vedic Mitra is associated with contracts, sunrise, harvest fertility, and truth. The Mitra-Varuna pairing connects Mitra with the sky god Varuna. Persian Mithra rides a bright chariot drawn by white horses. His weapons include a silver spear, a bow and golden arrows, daggers, axes, and a mace. Mithra bestows farr, divine grace, on a worthy monarch. Mithra’s name is invoked in Achaemenid inscriptions. The Mihr Yasht says Mithra stands for both the faithful and the unfaithful in contracts. The Mihr Yasht attributes swiftness for horses to Mithra. The hymn depicts Mithra with a thousand ears and ten thousand eyes. Mithra is praised as strong, sleepless, and always awake.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Ahriman adalah roh jahat dalam tradisi Iran, Zoroastrianisme, dan Zurvanisme, yang dikaitkan dengan kegelapan serta kekacauan.",
    "en": "Ahriman is an evil spirit in Iranian tradition, Zoroastrianism, and Zurvanism, associated with darkness and chaos.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "ahriman-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ahriman adalah roh jahat dalam tradisi Iran, Zoroastrianisme, dan Zurvanisme, yang dikaitkan dengan kegelapan serta kekacauan. Angra Mainyu menjadi nama lain Ahriman.",
      "en": "Ahriman is an evil spirit in Iranian tradition, Zoroastrianism, and Zurvanism, associated with darkness and chaos. Angra Mainyu is another name for Ahriman.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ahriman-c01",
        "ahriman-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sumber menjelaskan Ahriman berlawanan dengan Spenta Mainyu dan Ahura Mazda. Dalam Zurvanisme, Zurvan melahirkan Ahura Mazda dan Ahriman. Dalam uraian Hinnells yang dikutip, Ahriman mendiami jurang kegelapan tanpa akhir di utara. Ahriman dapat berubah bentuk menjadi kadal, ular, atau pemuda. Tujuan Ahriman dijelaskan sebagai menghancurkan ciptaan Ahura Mazda. Ahriman membalas kehidupan dengan kematian dan kesehatan dengan penyakit. Kisah yang dibahas melibatkan Gavaevodta, Banteng Primordial. Gayomartan juga dibunuh oleh Ahriman dalam kisah itu. Ahriman membisikkan kebohongan kepada pasangan manusia pertama bahwa dialah pencipta mereka. Nama Avestanya adalah Angra Mainyu. Dalam Gatha, angra mainyu belum diperlakukan sebagai nama diri. Pembacaan Ahuna Vairya oleh Ahura Mazda disebut membuat Angra Mainyu terpana. Pengantar terjemahan Vendidad I menjelaskan enam belas negeri ciptaan Ahura Mazda dan wabah tandingannya dari Angra Mainyu.",
      "en": "The source places Ahriman in opposition to Spenta Mainyu and Ahura Mazda. In Zurvanism, Zurvan gives birth to Ahura Mazda and Ahriman. In the quoted account by Hinnells, Ahriman inhabits an abyss of endless northern darkness. Ahriman can change form into a lizard, snake, or young man. Ahriman’s aim is described as destroying Ahura Mazda’s creation. Ahriman counters life with death and health with disease. The account includes Gavaevodta, the Primordial Bull. Gayomartan is also killed by Ahriman in that account. Ahriman lies to the first human couple by claiming to be their creator. His Avestan name is Angra Mainyu. In the Gathas, angra mainyu is not yet a proper name. Ahura Mazda’s recital of the Ahuna Vairya invocation puts Angra Mainyu into a stupor. The introduction to Vendidad I describes sixteen lands created by Ahura Mazda and opposing plagues created by Angra Mainyu.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Iblis adalah tokoh ajaran Islam yang dianggap pemimpin para setan. Al-Qur’an 18:50 menyebut Iblis berasal dari jin dan menolak perintah bersujud.",
      "en": "Iblis is an Islamic religious figure regarded as leader of the devils. Quran 18:50 identifies Iblis as one of the jinn who refuses the command to bow.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "iblis-c01",
        "iblis-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ayat itu menyebut Iblis dan keturunannya sebagai musuh manusia. Dalam Al-Qur’an 7:12, Iblis menyatakan dirinya lebih baik karena dibuat dari api, sedangkan Adam dari tanah liat. Hamid Algar mengaitkan kemungkinan asal kata Eblis dengan diabolos Yunani. Sebagian penafsir menghubungkan nama itu dengan ublisa dalam bahasa Arab. Algar menegaskan bahwa ayat 18:50 menyebut Eblis berasal dari jin. Banyak penulis Sufi yang dibahas menilai egoisme sebagai sumber kejatuhan Iblis. Penafsiran Sufi juga menyebut iri sebagai keburukan Iblis. Dalam uraian Sufi tersebut, Iblis menyesatkan manusia dengan menularkan sifat buruknya. Uraian itu menyebut Iblis dapat membujuk perbuatan baik untuk tujuan buruk. Bagi penulis Sufi yang dibahas, Eblis sebagai penggoda memiliki keberadaan di luar manusia. Sebagian ungkapan Sufi juga memperlakukan Eblis sebagai kecenderungan berdosa di dalam manusia. Algar mengutip al-Ghazali yang menyebut Iblis mengambil bentuk anjing, babi, dan katak. Algar menyebut Attar membuka kemungkinan pemulihan Iblis pada akhirnya.",
      "en": "The verse describes Iblis and his offspring as enemies of humanity. In Quran 7:12, Iblis claims superiority because he is created from fire and Adam from clay. Hamid Algar traces a possible origin of Eblis to Greek diabolos. Some interpreters connect the name with Arabic ublisa. Algar emphasizes that verse 18:50 identifies Eblis as being from the jinn. Many of the Sufi writers discussed regard egoism as the cause of Iblis’s downfall. Sufi interpretation also identifies envy as a vice of Eblis. In that Sufi account, Eblis misguides people by promoting his own vices. That account describes Eblis persuading people to perform good acts for malicious ends. For the Sufis discussed, Eblis as tempter has an existence external to humanity. Some Sufi expressions also treat Eblis as sinful inclinations internal to humanity. Algar cites al-Ghazali describing Eblis taking the forms of dogs, pigs, and frogs. Algar reports Attar suggesting Eblis may ultimately be rehabilitated.",

      "quote": "Iblis (bahasa Arab: إبليس, iblīs) merupakan tokoh dalam ajaran Islam yang dipandang sebagai pemimpin para setan.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",

    },
    {
      "id": "iblis-c02",
      "source_id": "iblis-s2",
      "quote": "they all bowed but Iblis. He was one of the jinni and rebelled against his Lord's command.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Al-Qur’an 18:50 menyebut Iblis berasal dari jin dan menolak perintah bersujud.",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iblis-c03",
      "source_id": "iblis-s2",
      "quote": "And yet you take him and his offspring as your friends instead of Me, even though they are your enemies.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ayat itu menyebut Iblis dan keturunannya sebagai musuh manusia.",
        "en": "The verse describes Iblis and his offspring as enemies of humanity."

    },
    {
      "id": "iblis-c04",
      "source_id": "iblis-s3",
      "quote": "\"I am better than him,\" said he. \"You created me from fire, and him from clay.\"",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Al-Qur’an 7:12, Iblis menyatakan dirinya lebih baik karena dibuat dari api, sedangkan Adam dari tanah liat.",
        "en": "In Quran 7:12, Iblis claims superiority because he is created from fire and Adam from clay."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iblis-c05",
      "source_id": "iblis-s4",
      "quote": "The word Eblīs, a Koranic designation for the devil, appears to derive ultimately from the Greek diabolos.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hamid Algar mengaitkan kemungkinan asal kata Eblis dengan diabolos Yunani.",

      "id": "iblis-c06",
      "source_id": "iblis-s4",
      "quote": "Some authorities have nonetheless imaginatively connected it with Arabic ublisa",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian penafsir menghubungkan nama itu dengan ublisa dalam bahasa Arab.",

      "source_id": "iblis-s4",

      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {

    {
      "id": "iblis-c08",
      "source_id": "iblis-s4",
      "quote": "Many of them regarded as the main source of his undoing the egoism",

      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",

    },
    {
      "id": "iblis-c09",
      "source_id": "iblis-s4",
      "quote": "Envy (ḥasad) is also discerned as one of the fatal vices of Eblīs",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 11",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penafsiran Sufi juga menyebut iri sebagai keburukan Iblis.",

    },
    {

      "source_id": "iblis-s4",
      "quote": "It is in large part by promoting his own vices among men that Eblīs deludes and misguides them",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 13",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam uraian Sufi tersebut, Iblis menyesatkan manusia dengan menularkan sifat buruknya.",

    },
    {
      "id": "iblis-c12",
      "source_id": "iblis-s4",
      "quote": "for the Sufis Eblīs, as the agent of temptation, has a real existence external to man.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 14",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bagi penulis Sufi yang dibahas, Eblis sebagai penggoda memiliki keberadaan di luar manusia.",

    {

      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian ungkapan Sufi juga memperlakukan Eblis sebagai kecenderungan berdosa di dalam manusia.",
        "en": "Some Sufi expressions also treat Eblis as sinful inclinations internal to humanity."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iblis-c14",
      "source_id": "iblis-s4",
      "quote": "Ḡazālī observes, however, that Eblīs sometimes assumes the form of animals such as dogs, pigs, and frogs",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 17",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Algar mengutip al-Ghazali yang menyebut Iblis mengambil bentuk anjing, babi, dan katak.",
        "en": "Algar cites al-Ghazali describing Eblis taking the forms of dogs, pigs, and frogs."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iblis-c15",
      "source_id": "iblis-s4",
      "quote": "ʿAṭṭār suggests that he may ultimately be rehabilitated",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 24",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Algar menyebut Attar membuka kemungkinan pemulihan Iblis pada akhirnya.",
        "en": "Algar reports Attar suggesting Eblis may ultimately be rehabilitated."
      }
    }
  ],

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Simurgh adalah burung mitos Persia yang juga disebut Senmurw. Nama Simurgh ditelusuri ke ungkapan Avesta burung Saena.",
      "en": "Simurgh is a Persian mythical bird also called Senmurw. The name Simurgh is traced to the Avestan expression for the bird Saena.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "simurgh-c01",
        "simurgh-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Pohon Saena dalam Yasht berada di tengah laut Vourukasha. Dalam Shahnameh Ferdowsi, Simurgh menjadi penyelamat, pendidik, dan penjaga Zal. Kamak disebut sebagai burung padanan jahat Senmurw. Sumber membedakan Simurgh pelindung Zal dan Rostam dari burung jahat bernama sama.",
      "en": "Saena’s tree in the Yasht stands in the sea Vourukasha. In Ferdowsi’s Shahnameh, Simurgh saves, teaches, and protects Zal. Kamak is identified as Senmurw’s evil bird counterpart. The source distinguishes the protector of Zal and Rostam from an evil bird with the same name.",

        "simurgh-c03",
        "simurgh-c04",

        "en": "Simurgh is a Persian mythical bird also called Senmurw."

      "source_id": "simurgh-s1",

    {
      "id": "simurgh-c04",
      "source_id": "simurgh-s1",

      "en": "Tiamat is a cosmic ocean paired with Abzu. Tiamat bears the first divine generation after mingling her waters with Abzu.",

        "tiamat-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Tiamat memilih Qingu sebagai pasangan baru dan memberinya Lauh Takdir. Marduk memotong tubuh Tiamat dan membentuk unsur kosmos darinya. Alexander Heidel memperingatkan bahwa bentuk naga Tiamat tidak dapat dipastikan. Tiamat dikalahkan dan dibunuh Marduk dalam pertempuran.",

      "source_id": "tiamat-s1",
      "quote": "Tiamat is the cosmic ocean that mates with Abzû, the groundwater",

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tiamat adalah samudra kosmis yang berpasangan dengan Abzu.",

      "source_id": "tiamat-s1",
      "quote": "She also takes a new consort, Qingu, and bestows on him the Tablet of Destinies",

      "en": "Nergal is a deity primarily associated with war, death, and disease. Kutha in northern Babylonia is Nergal’s main cult center.",

        "nergal-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kuil utama Nergal disebut E-Meslam. Nergal disinkretkan dengan Erra sejak masa awal. Nergal dan Ereshkigal serta Epos Erra merupakan dua mitos yang berpusat pada Nergal. Nergal digambarkan sebagai penguasa bersama Ereshkigal dan mula-mula dihubungkan dengan Shamash.",
      "en": "Nergal’s main temple is called E-Meslam. Nergal is syncretized with Erra at an early date. Nergal and Ereshkigal and the Epic of Erra are two myths focused on Nergal. Nergal is described as co-ruler with Ereshkigal and originally associated with Shamash.",

    "en": "Abzu, or Apsu, is a freshwater deity in Babylonian, Akkadian, and Sumerian traditions.",
    "claim_ids": [

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Abzu atau Apsu adalah dewa air tawar dalam tradisi Babilonia, Akkadia, dan Sumeria. Apsu atau Abzu dikisahkan mengelilingi bumi.",
      "en": "Abzu, or Apsu, is a freshwater deity in Babylonian, Akkadian, and Sumerian traditions. Apsu, or Abzu, is described as encircling the earth.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "abzu-c01",
        "abzu-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Abzu mencampurkan air tawarnya dengan air asin pasangan Tiamat. Dalam uraian itu, dewa-dewa lain lahir dari persatuan Abzu dan Tiamat. Ea membunuh Abzu sehingga memicu perang para dewa dengan Tiamat. Selain sebutan kosmologis, Abzu tampil sebagai dewa dalam Enuma Elish.",
      "en": "Abzu mingles his fresh waters with Tiamat’s salt waters. In that account, the other gods are born from Abzu and Tiamat’s union. Ea kills Abzu, provoking the gods’ war with Tiamat. Alongside its cosmological usage, Abzu appears as a deity in the Enuma Elish.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "abzu-c03",
        "abzu-c04",
        "abzu-c05",
        "abzu-c06"

      "id": "Al-Dimashqi menggambarkan ghoul sebagai penghuni gua yang keluar malam dan menghindari matahari. Dalam kisah Arabian Nights yang diterjemahkan, ghoul bersarang di bangunan kosong.       "id": "Moloch secara tradisional ditafsirkan sebagai nama dewa Kanaan. Peneliti juga mengusulkan Moloch sebagai sebutan pengorbanan, bukan nama dewa.",
      "en": "Moloch is traditionally interpreted as the name of a Canaanite deity. Scholars also propose that Moloch denotes a sacrifice rather than a deity.",

        "moloch-c01",
        "moloch-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Penafsiran sebagai pengorbanan semakin populer tetapi tetap diperdebatkan.       "en": "The sacrifice interpretation becomes more popular but remains disputed. Medieval depictions often give Moloch a bull-headed idol form with hands extended over fire.   "claims": [

      "id": "el-c01",
      "source_id": "el-s1",
      "quote": "was the supreme god of the ancient Canaanite religion.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "El adalah dewa tertinggi agama Kanaan kuno.",
        "en": "El is the supreme deity of ancient Canaanite religion."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "el-c02",
      "source_id": "el-s1",
      "quote": "El is often described as the father of the gods and the creator of humanity.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "El digambarkan sebagai ayah para dewa dan pencipta manusia.",
        "en": "El is described as father of the gods and creator of humanity."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "el-c03",
      "source_id": "el-s1",
      "quote": "El presided over a council of gods",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "El memimpin dewan para dewa.",
        "en": "El presides over a divine council."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "el-c04",
      "source_id": "el-s1",
      "quote": "El had many epithets, including \"Bull El,\" \"El the King,\" and \"Father of Mankind,\"",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gelar El mencakup Banteng El, El Sang Raja, dan Ayah Manusia.",
        "en": "El’s epithets include Bull El, El the King, and Father of Mankind."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "el-c05",
      "source_id": "el-s1",
      "quote": "In Northwest Semitic use, ʼel was a generic word for any god",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam bahasa Semit barat laut, el juga merupakan kata umum untuk dewa.",
        "en": "In Northwest Semitic usage, el also serves as a general word for a god."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "el-c06",
      "source_id": "el-s2",
      "quote": "Athirat, the wife of El and a mother goddess as the mother of the gods.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Athirat disebut sebagai istri El dan ibu para dewa.",
        "en": "Athirat is named as El’s wife and the mother of the gods."
      }

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Anahita adalah dewi Iran. Anahita dipuji dalam Aban Yasht yang terdapat di Avesta.",
      "en": "Anahita is an Iranian goddess. Anahita is praised in the Aban Yasht of the Avesta.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "anahita-c01",
        "anahita-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Bahasa Persia Tengah menyebutnya Ardwisur Anahid. Anahita dihubungkan dengan kesuburan, kelahiran, dan kebijaksanaan. Kuil Anahita dibangun di dekat aliran air. Anahita dihubungkan dengan Mithra; merpati dan merak menjadi hewan sucinya.",
      "en": "Middle Persian renders her name Ardwisur Anahid. Anahita is associated with fertility, procreation, and wisdom. Anahita’s temples are built beside streams and watercourses. Anahita is associated with Mithra, with the dove and peacock identified as her sacred animals.",

        "anahita-c03",
        "anahita-c04",
        "anahita-c05",
        "anahita-c06"

      "statement": {
        "id": "Anahita adalah dewi Iran.",
        "en": "Anahita is an Iranian goddess."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anahita-c02",
      "source_id": "anahita-s1",
      "quote": "She is praised in the Aban Yasht, one of the Zoroastrian hymns contained in the Avesta",

      "id": "anahita-c03",
      "source_id": "anahita-s1",
      "quote": "In Middle Persian, her name was rendered Ardwisur Anahid.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",

        "id": "Bahasa Persia Tengah menyebutnya Ardwisur Anahid.",

      "id": "anahita-c04",
      "source_id": "anahita-s1",
      "quote": "Anahita is associated with fertility, procreation, and wisdom.",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anahita-c05",
      "source_id": "anahita-s1",
      "quote": "Temples to Anahita were built next to streams and other watercourses.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",

      "en": "Ashur, or Assur, is the supreme Assyrian deity. Ashur originates as the local deity of the city bearing his name.",

        "ashur-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ashur adalah dewa langit dan perang bergelar Penguasa Seluruh Langit. Raja Asyur menyerukan Ashur sebagai sekutu yang kuat. Penggambarannya mencakup pemanah berjubah bulu yang menaiki ular atau naga. Mackenzie menyebut Ashur kadang dipuja dalam kuil dewa-dewa lain.",

        "lamassu-c02"
      ]
    },

      "id": "lamassu-c01",

      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

        "en": "Lamassu is a Mesopotamian protective deity."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lamassu-c02",
      "source_id": "lamassu-s1",
      "quote": "Initially depicted as a goddess in Sumerian times",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada masa Sumeria, Lamma mula-mula digambarkan sebagai dewi.",
        "en": "In Sumerian times, Lamma is initially depicted as a goddess."

      "source_id": "lamassu-s1",

        "ninurta-c02"
      ]

      "quote": "Some scholars believe she was seen as early as the 2nd millennium BCE as a popular female deity in the Hittite culture.",

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian peneliti menelusuri pemujaannya dalam budaya Het hingga milenium kedua SM.",

    {

      "source_id": "asherah-s2",
      "quote": "There is a debate as to whether these inscriptions refer to Asherah the goddess or an asherah as cultic object.",

        "en": "The inscriptions discussed are debated as referring to the goddess Asherah or a cultic object."

      "claim_ids": [
        "daeva-c01",
        "daeva-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Avesta Muda menjadikan daeva sosok ilahi pembawa kekacauan. Daeva dan deva Hindu memiliki asal kata yang sama. Bentuk bahasa Iran lainnya mencakup dew dalam Kurdi dan div dalam Persia. Pernyataan keagamaan dalam Mihr Yasht menolak daeva dan menaati hukum Ahura.",
      "en": "In the Younger Avesta, daevas promote chaos and disorder. Iranian daeva and Hindu deva share a linguistic origin. Iranian-language equivalents include Kurdish dew and Persian div. The religious profession in the Mihr Yasht rejects daevas and follows Ahura’s laws.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Roc adalah burung raksasa dalam mitologi.",
    "en": "The roc is a giant mythological bird.",

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Roc adalah burung raksasa dalam mitologi. Dalam Arabian Nights, roc digambarkan sebagai burung putih yang mampu membawa gajah ke sarangnya di bukit.",
      "en": "The roc is a giant mythological bird. In Arabian Nights, the roc is a white bird able to carry an elephant to its hillside nest.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "roc-mythology-c01",
        "roc-mythology-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Roc dalam kisah itu membawa narator ke puncak gunung dengan cakar besar. Dalam perjalanan Sindbad, burung itu menutupi telur dengan sayap untuk menghangatkannya. Sindbad mengikat dirinya pada kaki burung memakai kain serban. Dalam perjalanan berikutnya, dua roc membawa batu besar untuk menghancurkan kapal.",
      "en": "The tale’s roc carries the narrator to a mountaintop in its huge claws. In Sindbad’s voyage, the bird covers its egg with its wings to keep it warm. Sindbad ties himself to the bird’s foot using his turban cloth. In a later voyage, two rocs carry huge rocks to crush the ship.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "roc-mythology-c03",
        "roc-mythology-c04",
        "roc-mythology-c05",
        "roc-mythology-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "roc-mythology-c01",
      "source_id": "roc-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "Roc adalah sebuah makhluk mitologi yang berwujud burung raksasa",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Roc adalah burung raksasa dalam mitologi.",
        "en": "The roc is a giant mythological bird."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "roc-mythology-c02",
      "source_id": "roc-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "this great white bird is so strong that he has been known to carry even an elephant to his nest in the hills.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 531",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Arabian Nights, roc digambarkan sebagai burung putih yang mampu membawa gajah ke sarangnya di bukit.",
        "en": "In Arabian Nights, the roc is a white bird able to carry an elephant to its hillside nest."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "roc-mythology-c03",
      "source_id": "roc-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "In a few minutes the roc appeared, and bore me off to the top of the mountain in his huge claws",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 531",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Roc dalam kisah itu membawa narator ke puncak gunung dengan cakar besar.",
        "en": "The tale’s roc carries the narrator to a mountaintop in its huge claws."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "roc-mythology-c04",
      "source_id": "roc-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "Sure enough the bird settled slowly down upon it, covering it with its wings to keep it warm",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 582",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam perjalanan Sindbad, burung itu menutupi telur dengan sayap untuk menghangatkannya.",
        "en": "In Sindbad’s voyage, the bird covers its egg with its wings to keep it warm."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "roc-mythology-c05",
      "source_id": "roc-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "Taking off my turban I bound myself securely to it with the linen",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 582",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sindbad mengikat dirinya pada kaki burung memakai kain serban.",
        "en": "Sindbad ties himself to the bird’s foot using his turban cloth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "roc-mythology-c06",
      "source_id": "roc-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "we saw that each held in its claws an immense rock ready to crush us.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 639",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam perjalanan berikutnya, dua roc membawa batu besar untuk menghancurkan kapal.",
        "en": "In a later voyage, two rocs carry huge rocks to crush the ship."
      }
    }
  ],

      "id": "Tanit dihubungkan dengan kebijaksanaan, peradaban, dan kerajinan. Tanit dipercaya melindungi kota dan rumah tempat ia dipuja. Tanit juga dihubungkan dengan hujan. Di Afrika Utara, Tanit kemudian tetap dipuja dengan nama Latin Juno Caelestis.",

      "id": "Humbaba adalah tokoh mitologi Mesopotamia. Humbaba digambarkan sebagai sosok mirip manusia yang dibandingkan dengan ogre atau raksasa.",
      "en": "Humbaba is a figure in Mesopotamian mythology. Humbaba is depicted as an anthropomorphic figure comparable to an ogre or giant.",

        "humbaba-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Asal dan arti nama Humbaba tidak diketahui. Nama Humbaba pertama ditemukan sebagai nama pribadi dalam dokumen masa Ur III. Humbaba menjaga Hutan Aras dan dibunuh Gilgamesh serta Enkidu dalam eposnya. Humbaba digambarkan berbulu, bercakar singa, dan berwajah monster.",
      "en": "The origin and meaning of Humbaba’s name are unknown. Humbaba first occurs as a personal name in documents from the Ur III period. Humbaba guards the Cedar Forest and is killed by Gilgamesh and Enkidu in the epic. Humbaba is depicted as hairy, with lion’s claws and a monstrous face.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Utu, juga disebut Shamash atau Babbar, adalah dewa matahari dan keadilan Sumeria.",
    "en": "Utu, also called Shamash or Babbar, is the Sumerian deity of sun and justice.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "shamash-q526992-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Utu, juga disebut Shamash atau Babbar, adalah dewa matahari dan keadilan Sumeria. Shamash Akkadia diidentifikasi dengan dewa matahari Sumeria Utu.",
      "en": "Utu, also called Shamash or Babbar, is the Sumerian deity of sun and justice. Akkadian Shamash is identified with the earlier Sumerian sun god Utu.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "shamash-q526992-c01",
        "shamash-q526992-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Aya atau Sherida, dewi fajar, disebut sebagai pasangannya. Sumber menelusuri Utu sebagai salah satu dewa Mesopotamia tertua, sekitar 3500 SM. Padanan Akkadianya, Shamash, menguasai keadilan tertinggi. Nanna dan Ningal disebut sebagai orang tua Utu-Shamash.",
      "en": "Aya, or Sherida, the dawn goddess, is his consort. The source traces Utu to around 3500 BCE as one of the oldest Mesopotamian deities. His Akkadian counterpart, Shamash, governs supreme justice. Nanna and Ningal are identified as Utu-Shamash’s parents.",

      "id": "Hippocamp adalah makhluk mitos yang menggabungkan tubuh atas kuda dan tubuh bawah ikan. Koin Tirus abad keempat SM menggambarkan Melqart menaiki hippocamp bersayap.",

Bentuknya menggabungkan kepala dan bagian depan kuda dengan ekor ikan menyerupai ular. Seni mosaik dapat memberi hippocamp sisik hijau serta surai dan anggota mirip sirip.",
